- events-context-index.json now backfills commit/changed_files from git history (Run-ID: commit trailer) and recomputes area_ids from them, replacing the unused extraction field with per-field provenance and a coverage summary
- Merged sweep_outbox's duplicate git-state predicates (op-in-progress, staged-changes) into the shared main_tree_guards module, closing a latent gap where an unrunnable git binary raised instead of failing closed
- windows-tests.yml now provisions its pytest environment once (was 3x) and runs shared/tests under pytest-xdist (-n 4), cutting the job from ~24-28min to a predicted ~10-14min
- decision-drops/ is now tracked in git (this repo, the shared gitignore template, and shipwright-webui) instead of gitignored, so ADR content ships with the branch/PR that produced it instead of depending on which machine happens to run the next release

### Removed

- Multi-session pipeline mode and its external per-phase-session engine. `/shipwright-run` used to hand each phase off to its own `claude --session-id` session, driven by three hooks (`phase_session_start`, `phase_user_prompt_validate`, `phase_session_stop`) wired into all 8 phase plugins — so the pipeline could not advance in the VS Code extension or desktop chat, which cannot spawn a bound session. Those hooks (and their helpers `phase_context_blocks`, `hook_session`, `phase_event_emit`) are deleted and deregistered.
- Dead `_TRIVYIGNORE_NAMES` constant in `ci_security.py`, left behind by the Phase-1 extraction of the widened view into `accepted_risk_scan.py`. It was the repo's only open code-scanning alert, and it was a fix rather than a risk to accept.
- The `security:` job from the Supabase/Next.js CI template. It ran `AikidoSec/github-actions-wf@v1`, an action whose repository no longer exists (404), so the job could never have run for anyone adopting this template — and it additionally demanded an `AIKIDO_SECRET_KEY` secret nobody holds. Security scanning for adopted repos lives in `security.yml`, which `/shipwright-adopt` scaffolds independently, so this removes a false claim of coverage rather than coverage.
- Five documents recording artifact migrations this repository completed in April and May 2026, and one command-line helper that announced the next pending migration. All four migrations have been marked complete for months, so the helper only ever printed "nothing pending" and was wired into no hook or skill; the records described work that will not repeat. Git history keeps all of them. The path-canon exemption that existed solely to tolerate the legacy paths those documents quoted was removed after verifying the check still passes without it.
- The event-derived `Total review findings` / `Unresolved findings` rows are gone from the traceability matrix. The section-derived rows, which have a real fixed-list behind them, are unchanged.
- Retired the producerless runtime PID verifier and its obsolete unified-verifier CLI phase after the WebUI process governor was removed.

### Fixed

- Pipeline phase skills (project/design/plan/build/test/changelog/deploy) now detect pipeline-vs-standalone mode from the orchestrator's dispatch token instead of the v1 run-config `current_step` field the v2 pipeline never advances — a driven phase no longer misclassifies itself as standalone and mis-stamps its artifacts
- `orchestrator.py update-step` is now inert inside a driven single-session run, so a phase skill can no longer wedge a structurally healthy pipeline into a stuck `needs_validation` state
- **Triage sweep no longer destroys operator dismisses.** An append written into main's tracked `triage.jsonl` while uncommitted reached no branch and no origin, so a `status` for it looked like an orphan — the quarantine deleted the operator's dismiss and reported success, and the item resurrected on the board after every dismiss (shipwright-webui, trg-6db81c59). The sweep now adopts such append-only drift into the outbox (the real delivery channel) and restores main's tracked log to HEAD, and `sweep_quarantine.decide()` takes the append ids known from main's log so a legitimate status is never quarantined. It plans before it mutates, refuses any main-tree state it does not understand, and surfaces quarantine + adoption counts to the operator.
- **`/shipwright-adopt`: `git.major_refactor_commits` was always empty, for every repository.** `git log --numstat` prints a blank line between a commit's header and its file stats; the parser treated that blank as end-of-commit and reset its state before a single stat line was read, so the "5+ files changed" threshold was unreachable. Refactor commits are now detected, and the header fields are separated by `0x1F` so a `|` in a commit subject no longer shifts the recorded date and author.
- Iterate merges no longer abort when `.shipwright/compliance/ci-security.json` conflicts — the derived CI-security summary is admitted to the churn-merge allowlist and resolved like the compliance snapshots, with matching regenerate-failure rollback parity.
- The iterate decision-log (`write_decision_drop.py`) and changelog (`write_changelog_drop.py`) drop-writers are now idempotent per `(run_id, content)` — a re-run with identical content returns the existing drop instead of writing a duplicate `_NNN` entry (first-run output is byte-identical).
- finalize_iterate.py no longer crashes with UnicodeEncodeError on Windows (cp1252) when its stdout/stderr is a pipe and the emitted JSON or diagnostics carry non-cp1252 characters (e.g. a repo under a CJK/Cyrillic path), which previously aborted the whole finalize bundle
- shipwright-test: stop the performance-check integration test from writing .shipwright/triage.jsonl into the tracked tests/fixtures dir — it now points the runner at a throwaway tmp_path, so serial test runs no longer leave the tree dirty
- The changelog aggregator no longer appends a duplicate `ADR-NNN` bullet for a change whose run_id bullet already exists — ending the run_id/ADR duplication in the always-loaded agent docs; the direct decision-log path now emits the same canonical form.
- The shared backfill engine now scans correctly when run inside an iterate worktree — previously a `.worktrees/` ancestor false-pruned every test file, so it found nothing.
- Churn-merge regenerate now stages .shipwright/compliance/ci-security.json in the integrate follow-up commit, so a fresh security.yml scan's summary refresh during an integrate merge is no longer left modified-but-unstaged (forward mirror of the rollback restore set).
- `gh` output is now decoded as UTF-8 explicitly. `text=True` alone decodes with the locale codec, which is cp1252 on Windows and raises on the first non-Latin-1 byte in an alert body — silently turning every GitHub triage import into a failed fetch.
- Merge reconciliation now auto-resolves a conflicted `.shipwright/compliance/test-traceability.json` during `origin/main` integration (it was missing from the churn allowlist, so `ensure_current`/`integrate_main` aborted and required a manual conflict resolution). Mirrors the existing `ci-security.json` handling — placeholder at conflict, then re-derived + staged in the follow-up commit.
- The event log no longer loses records to a damaged line. When two events ended up on the same physical line — which an ordinary git union merge can cause when one side's file lacks a trailing newline — the reader skipped the whole line and BOTH events disappeared. Because that log is the audit trail, a lost work_completed made a step that really happened read as one that never did. Records on such a line are now recovered in order, and the event writers check for an unterminated line before appending. Extends the same fix already shipped for the triage log.
- The finalization requirement gate now rejects a declared requirement id that exists in no spec. Previously any non-empty text passed and a non-existent id was only reported after merge, by a non-blocking check.
- Retiring a requirement can no longer be quietly waved through by also adding a fold row for it: tests still labelled with a removed requirement are still reported, so removing a feature continues to force a decision about its tests.
- A requirements table that lists fold aliases without backticks is no longer misread as declaring those old IDs as live requirements (which would have demanded test coverage for requirements that no longer exist).
- Shared compliance modules now load reliably regardless of which plugin's code is on the import path first — previously a shared module could fail to load with `ModuleNotFoundError` depending on the order tests happened to run in.
- Triage log: a record written without a trailing newline let the next writer append onto the same line, and the reader discarded BOTH records — so an operator's dismissal could silently fail to propagate. Writers now guarantee termination and readers recover all records on a shared line.
- `astral-sh/setup-uv` is now SHA-pinned in the shipped CI template, to the same commit the framework already pinned for its own workflows. The framework had been holding itself to a stricter standard than it shipped.
- The requirement-existence gate shipped on 2026-07-18 was missing its one-line entry in the conventions record, which had been failing the consistency check that verifies every architectural decision is written down where it belongs.
- Event-log readers no longer discard records that share one physical line. Eleven readers — the compliance change-history collector, the Group B/D audits, FR-traceability backfill, boundary-coverage reporting and the F11 verifiers — now delegate record-boundary parsing to the shared SSoT, so a `work_completed` that is present on disk stops reading as absent.
- The F11 `check_events_has_commit` gate no longer fails finalization for a correctly-recorded run. Its oracle skipped any physical line holding more than one record, so an iterate whose `work_completed` landed second on such a line read as never recorded.
- Four latent crash paths in the event-log readers: a bare JSON scalar line raised `AttributeError` in `events_log` and the `iterate_checks` lookups, and undecodable bytes raised `UnicodeDecodeError` past an `except OSError` that could never catch it.
- Two compliance-audit checks stopped reporting a clean result about a set they never examined. The layer-coverage detective (D-layer) used to report "every active FR is covered at its required layers" when the traceability manifest declared no requirements at all — indistinguishable, to a reader, from a genuinely fully-covered project; it now skips with a detail naming the state, which does not affect the audit's exit code. The stale-reference detective (D2) used to skip when a project had no readable requirements, which is exactly the state in which every requirement reference in the change log is broken; it now reports those references. **D2's change can newly fail an audit:** a project with no readable requirements AND at least one recorded change naming a requirement now exits non-zero, and the dashboard reads "FAIL — drift found". D2 still skips when there are no references to check, so a project that simply has not written requirements yet is unaffected. Where requirements exist but their table could not be read, the report now names the table-shape defect instead of accusing the operator of stale references. See `docs/migrations/requirements-catalog-merge.md`.
- Adopt no longer emits the non-canonical FR-01.100 past 99 detected routes — generated requirement ids now roll the group over (FR-01.99 -> FR-02.01) so every id stays canonical.
- `/shipwright-adopt` no longer records a page found only by browsing the running app as though it had been read from source code — an unmatched placeholder value was being treated as real evidence. It also escapes special characters when writing the requirements table, so a description containing a `|` can no longer break the table apart and shift a requirement's text into the wrong column.
- Event-log readers in the adopt, grade and compliance-verifier paths, plus the churn-merge integrate-main validator, now recover records that share one physical line after a union merge — so a record sitting second on a concatenated line (an adoption event, a run's work_completed, the newest work commit) is no longer read as absent.
- Flaky finalize idempotency test no longer reddens PRs that run across a UTC minute boundary — the dashboard-render comparison excludes the wall-clock banner minute (trg-183a304a).
- Two specs declaring the same FR id now fail compliance regeneration with an error naming both spec paths, instead of silently dropping one requirement from the traceability manifest.
- The traceability matrix could display the wrong requirement text when a table row carried more cells than its header declared, and a compliance check reported "nothing to audit" instead of a failure when it could not read a populated spec.
- A requirement description containing a file path, a regex, or an escaped `|` character is no longer mangled when read back out of a spec table.
- Iterate finalization no longer silently loses the per-run ADR or iterate-entry record: the sub-iterate-runner contract now runs F3 (decision-drop) and F5c (iterate entry) as mandatory steps and self-verifies them before push.
- The shallow-clone anti-skip guard in the FR-history provenance meta-test now catches `pytest.skip`'s exception by name rather than swallowing every `BaseException`, so `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` are no longer converted into test failures — and the guard itself is exercised for the first time, in a child pytest process.
- Traceability change-history tests now fail loudly on a shallow clone (naming `fetch-depth: 0`) instead of skipping silently, closing a fail-open gap (trg-3a131594).
- Compliance D1/D3 no longer report a requirement as uncovered or undelivered merely because it was introduced rather than later revised. A change that mints a requirement and ran tests now counts as covering and delivering it, matching what the write-side FR gate has always accepted.
- A non-dict `tests` value in the event log no longer crashes the Group-D checks into a synthetic HIGH finding.
- Design compliance check (detective-audit C1) no longer reports a false failure for adopted/brownfield projects that have requirements but never ran the design/mockup phase — the FR→screen coverage check now skips when the design phase is absent from the project lifecycle, mirroring the existing library-scope skip.
- D4 no longer reports an FR as landing in a failing build when its latest covering event merely skipped host-gated tests; the detective is re-enabled on the framework monorepo now that its false-positive root cause is fixed.
- A consistency audit that ran no checks — a mistyped `--only` group, for instance — is no longer recorded as a passing run. Previously it was stored as `PASS` with zero checks and replaced the record of the last real audit, so the compliance documents reported a fresh clean cross-check that had verified nothing. The earlier record now survives untouched, and the operator is told on stderr that nothing ran.
- The phase-completion check on the session handover note now asks whether the note belongs to the run being checked, instead of how recently the file was written — so a run that spent a long time waiting on the build service is no longer reported as stale, and a note left behind by an earlier run is now caught.
- Canon C3 now reports a phase that finished without leaving a handover note, instead of passing it silently. It asks whether that phase itself wrote the note, rather than trusting a run identifier several phases share.
- A handover note written mid-build, per section, or at the end of an iterate no longer erases the marker that records which phase closed last — which had been blanking the freshness check for all eight pipeline phases at once.
- Re-running a release no longer adds a second entry for the same version. The changelog is written before the pending entries are cleared, so a release interrupted in between used to leave both behind and produce a duplicate section; it now updates the entry already there and clears the pending items.
- A release stops instead of overwriting a version entry it cannot safely reproduce — one that no longer matches the pending items (an earlier attempt having cleared some of them, or a hand edit), one marked in a way rewriting would erase such as a withdrawal notice, or a file already holding two entries for that version. Nothing is changed and nothing is cleared, so the two can be reconciled by hand.
- Pending entries left over from the older way of writing release notes are now reported however the pending-section heading is capitalised; a lower-case spelling used to be silently skipped, so the operator was never told those entries were being left behind.
- **changelog:** `update_changelog` no longer destroys the file it was asked to extend. It now splices the new release note into the existing document instead of rebuilding it from a fresh header, so a hand-written history — the normal case for a repo onboarded with its own CHANGELOG — keeps its title and every previous entry. Pending `[Unreleased]` bullets are preserved too (a second, unreported loss found while probing). Closes trg-6690d175 (critical); FR-01.09.
- **changelog:** re-running an interrupted release no longer duplicates a version. Because tagging is instruction rather than code, a run that stopped after writing the note left it unmarked; the next run appended the same section again. The writer now replaces that version's section, so one version appears exactly once.
- **changelog:** the release note writer now preserves a UTF-8 BOM and the file's own line endings (an LF-authored CHANGELOG.md was being rewritten wholesale to CRLF on Windows), bounds a replaced section at what it actually owns so trailing prose and the Keep-a-Changelog link-reference footer survive a re-run, and writes atomically so an interrupted write cannot truncate the history.
- Two verification scripts that were referenced by no workflow — the cross-repo contract surface and the triage sweep-delivery surface — now run in CI's required job, so a drift they detect blocks the merge instead of going unnoticed.
- Parallel change-runs no longer block each other at merge time. The generated compliance and dashboard views are now produced from the main branch after a change lands, instead of each run committing its own copy — those copies were both the sole cause of the conflicts and, because they were computed from a partial view of history, wrong about the project.
- The session handoff's `## Pipeline Phases` block no longer overstates progress: the finished tally counts against the real pipeline (including per-split expansion and legacy phases outside it) instead of against the phase tasks created so far, which made a run one phase into seven read as "1 of 2" and a fully-built three-split run read as 100% complete
- The handoff no longer calls a phase "Currently dispatched" without evidence — a pointer resting on a not-yet-started successor now reads "Next up", a recovered task is not reported as in flight because its attempt counter survived recovery, and a finished or failed task under the pointer is labelled terminal instead of live
- A phase step with no validator (`security`) now records `gate_result: "not_checked"` in `validation_overrides[]` instead of `"pass"`, which had asserted that a gate was satisfied where no gate exists
- Completing a phase after a paused one no longer leaves the run reporting `needs_validation` with no findings left on disk to explain it, and a pause set by the phase-task lifecycle is no longer silently lifted by a v1 step completion
- A crash in the optional Phase-Quality critical gate no longer discards the findings the phase validator already produced, a gate crash now writes its traceback to stderr, and inform-level notes no longer duplicate into the tracked build dashboard on a pause-then-override retry
- When a change is waiting to be merged, the report now names the code host's own reason — the branch conflicts with what it is merging into, it is still a draft, its base has moved on, or the host refuses it — instead of only recognising one of those and describing the rest as nothing being wrong. A reason the host gives that we do not recognise is reported as not understood rather than as fine.
- The check that the session handoff describes the current run now reads the handoff instead of the clock. It no longer warns on runs that simply spent a long time waiting for checks to finish.
- Recording a change as a new acceptance criterion on an existing requirement — the way this project asks such changes to be recorded — is now attributed to that requirement and checked at the levels it demands, instead of being reported as impossible to determine. A change that touches no requirement at all is still reported as undetermined for a person to settle.
- An outside reviewer that answers without delivering a review — nothing at all, or an answer the provider itself reports as cut off — is now recorded as having failed, with the reason, rather than as having reviewed.
- Overriding a phase validation gate no longer skips the check: `orchestrator.py update-step --force` runs the validator either way, requires `--force-reason "<why>"`, and records what the gate found together with the reason in `shipwright_run_config.json` → `validation_overrides[]` — so a phase that passed its checks stays distinguishable from one that was waved through
- /shipwright-plan: a malformed SECTION_MANIFEST — duplicate section ids, an invalid dependency id, or an empty dependency entry — now reports the offending line and fails, instead of the surviving entries being able to report success.
- shipwright-security: when the Tier-3 review cannot read a whole PR it now names the files it did not review — separating those left out entirely from the at-most-one supplied only as context — instead of reporting a character count. Files whose path cannot be parsed are disclosed as a count rather than silently missing, and every path is sanitised before reaching the model prompt, the PR comment or CI logs.
- The pull-request reviewer no longer spends its reading budget on a run's own review transcripts. `reviews.json` and the raw external-review replies are records *of* a review, so reviewing them is circular — and on a large change they were 19% of what the reviewer read, which was the difference between reviewing the change and refusing it as too big. The iterate spec and mini-plan stay reviewable: they state the acceptance criteria the diff should be read against.
- The external code review is no longer chained behind the internal one. It used to fire only if the internal reviewer had fired, which put both behind a single point of failure: if the internal pass could not run for any reason, the external pass was excused along with it and a larger change could finish with no code review at all. It now fires on its own conditions, and when the internal reviewer cannot run the responsibility moves outward instead of lapsing — the external review becomes mandatory and carries the pass. Decoupling also exposed a contradiction the chain had been hiding: a small change touching security-sensitive files both did and did not require the cascade. The exemption for small changes now applies only when none of the thresholds is met.
- **Rolling back now actually uses the version you ask for.** `/shipwright-deploy`'s git rollback took a target version and then redeployed whatever was at the top of the branch — after a bad release, the bad code — while reporting success and naming the version it never sent. It now pins that version onto the hosting target before redeploying, reads it back, and reports what the target confirmed rather than what was intended.
- **A slow start-up is no longer reported as a failed release.** The liveness check asked once with a ten-second limit and never retried, so an application taking fifteen seconds to boot triggered an unnecessary rollback. It now keeps asking until the app answers or a deadline passes, and the deadline comes from the hosting target's own profile. Callers that set no deadline still make exactly one attempt.
- Windows: a concurrent reader no longer costs a writer its write, and a concurrent writer no longer costs a reader its read. `os.replace` is atomic on Windows only when it succeeds, so every `shipwright_*` config/state/log write (30 call sites) could fail outright whenever any process — the WebUI, an editor, an indexer, antivirus — held the file open. Both directions now retry a sharing violation for a bounded budget and still fail loudly past it.
- The release CLI reports why it stopped instead of crashing the caller. On a pipe the child picked the locale codec (cp1252 on Windows) while every reader decodes UTF-8, so one non-ASCII character in a refusal message — or merely a project under a non-ASCII path — killed the caller's reader thread and turned the explanation into `stderr = None`.
- The security plugin's own test run no longer leaves stray files in the project. Two tests drove the report generator without giving it a throwaway working directory, so it wrote a triage store — holding test dummies that read like real security findings, plus a lock file — into the plugin folder, where git does not ignore it. Anyone checking in everything after a test run committed them. A session-end guard now fails the suite that causes it.
- A browser test that failed and then passed on a retry is now recorded as a pass and counted separately, so a test that has needed a retry for weeks becomes visible before it fails for good. Each test also counts once however many attempts it took — retries previously inflated the total and marked a retried-and-passed test as failed, turning a green run red.
- A triage entry built from a failing GitHub check no longer carries an unbounded detail line. It is capped at 1024 characters like the security and pull-request entries, so one entry with a very long workflow or branch name cannot crowd the others out of a view that shows a limited number of items.
- A deferred triage entry whose reason was present but blank — only spaces, or an invisible character — printed an empty `reason:` line instead of "(no reason recorded)". The placeholder now depends on whether anything visible survives, not on whether the field is non-empty.
- The iterate's closing banner (F12) gained a conditional `Reviews:` row naming a cascade that did not run. The instruction to surface it existed but pointed at a template with no such field, so an operator reading the summary learned nothing about the missing pass.
- A standalone iterate now spawns the spec-reviewer -> code-reviewer -> doubt-reviewer cascade itself before committing, instead of delegating it to a document that pointed back at the delegation. ADR-029's 'the runner has no Agent tool' is marked campaign-only wherever it appears, so it can no longer be read as telling every iterate not to review.
- A campaign sub-iterate records each review pass under the name of whoever performed it: the external review as external_code, the delegated internal cascade as not_run with the capability limit spelled out. Previously one marker closed `code` as completed on the strength of the external run, so the record claimed an internal review that never happened.
- A change touching `.github/workflows/**` can satisfy both F11 gates at once again. The CI supply-chain acknowledgement now lives at `.shipwright/planning/iterate/<run_id>/ci_supplychain_ack.json` instead of inside the derived `shipwright_test_results.json`, which the iterate commit may not carry and which finalization reset mid-run.
- Committed file content is read by blob OID rather than `git show <rev>:<path>`, which git first stats as a filename and so crosses Windows' 260-character path limit on deep checkouts. A failed read is now an error instead of being hashed as the "file deleted" sentinel.
- `test_checks_that_gate.py` no longer carries a `security.yml` constant no assertion reads: the security half was split out to `test_security_gate_verdict.py` and only the docstring bullet was left behind. The console verdict line is now pinned there too, scoped to the step log so relocating it into the job summary cannot pass unnoticed.
- Finalizing a change now checks evidence that belongs to the run being finalized. The file holding a run's test-completeness ledger, its end-to-end result and its declared removals is rebuilt from the main branch during finalization, so three checks were reading the PREVIOUS run's numbers and reporting them as this one's — one of them could let another run's stated exception excuse a removal nobody declared here. Each check now refuses evidence that does not name this run, and says which run it found instead and how to repair it.
- Three narrowings to the check that stops a branch quietly dropping work merged by someone else: it now shares one definition of 'regenerated file' with the merge resolver, compares against the remote default branch when no local copy exists instead of skipping the comparison, and uses one rule for whether two lines are the same, so a reformat no longer produces a finding nothing can answer.
- A Control-Grade snapshot taken in a work branch is no longer labelled as the main branch's own. Previously a branch measured before its first commit was recorded as if it described main, which is the exact confusion the attribution was added to prevent.
- A recorded grade measurement can no longer have its origin rewritten after the fact through the event-correction command, and the documentation no longer claims guarantees the code did not provide.
- Control-Grade snapshots now record which tree they were measured in (lineage, branch, and the merge-base with the default branch), so a grade trend can be filtered to one lineage instead of mixing measurements from different worktrees into a single misleading series.
- The release gate named for security now reads the security scan. `check_security_scan` moved off the RTM's `Unresolved findings` row — code-review findings summed over `work_completed` events, which only 4 of 399 events feed and which can never catch up because `review.fixed` is written before the remediation commits exist — onto `.shipwright/compliance/ci-security.json`. It fails closed on a malformed, non-regular, degraded, or unsizeable summary and fails open only when no scan exists at all.
- An iterate's test totals now reach its `work_completed` event. `finalize_iterate` (F5b) folds `iterate_latest` from `shipwright_test_results.json` onto the event, guarded on `run_id` so a stale derived snapshot cannot launder another run's numbers. Compliance D1 keys FR coverage on `tests.total > 0`, so the worktree flow had been reporting the recorder's silence as the project's coverage gap.
- Canon C3 now checks the iterate phase against its own clock, not only its run id: the F5c ledger writer stamps the `event_at` anchor from the same source as the handover note's canon marker, so an iterate that completed again without re-writing its note is reported instead of passing silently.
- Onboarding an existing repo with a load-bearing `CLAUDE.md` now actually delivers the standing request: `write_claude_md` appends the section instead of writing its render to a side-file no harness loads. The append is idempotent, leaves the original bytes untouched, and is disclosed in `preservation_log.json`.
- The check that refuses a hand-over when work merged by someone else would be undone no longer reports two things that are not losses: content the finished work itself removed again afterwards, and a line this change edited rather than deleted. It had raised four such reports in a single change, each waved through with a written reason — a safety check whose exception becomes routine stops being a safety check. It still refuses the case it was built for, and now says which version of the finished work it compared against.
- The check that keeps generated compliance documents out of a pull request no longer passes blind: it reads what the whole branch changed instead of only the last commit, so files that slipped in earlier are caught.
- A branch is no longer blamed for generated files that arrived from the main branch, even in a clone whose recorded default branch is stale.
- An iterate's own test ledger (`shipwright_test_results.json`) is no longer wiped by the F11 integration: it is carried across the merge instead of reset to HEAD, so the run's evidence survives a merge, an abort, and a mainline deletion.
- `integrate` no longer reports "merge aborted" over a repository that is still mid-merge — all three abort paths now verify that `MERGE_HEAD` actually cleared.
- Adding a requirement can pass CI again: two integration tests asserted equality against artifacts #480 froze, which left a minting branch no passing option.
- Accepted-risk drift gate: an absent `shipwright_accepted_risks.yaml` no longer bypasses reconciliation. A fresh repo still passes (it suppresses nothing), but deleting the register in a repo that HAS suppressions now reports every one as `UNRECORDED` instead of exiting 0 silently.
- Accepted-risk drift gate: a Trivy ignore entry whose own expiry has lapsed no longer counts as an active suppression. Both ignore-file forms are honoured (`expired_at:` in YAML, `exp:YYYY-MM-DD` in the classic flat form), mirroring Trivy's own rule, so renewing only the register's date now reports `STALE` instead of reading as reconciled.
- The classic flat `.trivyignore` reader no longer returns the whole line as the finding id, so an entry carrying `exp:` can match a register rule at all.
- Two iterates running in parallel that each add an architecture decision no longer block each other. The generated ADR index is now reconciled automatically when their branches merge — both entries survive, rebuilt from the merged folder — instead of stopping the merge and waiting for someone to resolve it by hand.
- The ADR index (`.shipwright/planning/adr/INDEX.md`) now refreshes when an iterate records its ADR, so a new ADR appears in the index in the same commit that adds it. Previously the index was only rebuilt as a side-effect of a release pass that had decision-drops to fold, so ADRs written by an iterate could stay unlisted indefinitely (10 were, here).
- The derived-snapshot gate's printed remedy no longer sends the operator in a circle. On a merge HEAD — the shape the gate was taught to catch — `--source=HEAD~1` named the branch's own pre-merge tip, i.e. the commit carrying the offending snapshot, so following the instruction changed nothing and the next run failed identically. The restore source is now the branch's merge-base with the default branch, and when no base resolves the gate prints no command at all rather than a wrong one.
- **An iterate now gets its pull request merged even where GitHub cannot merge it for you.** On a repository whose main branch has no protection — which includes every private repo on the free plan — GitHub refuses to set up automatic merging at all, so previously every single iterate ended with an open, green, unmerged PR after half an hour of waiting for a merger that could not exist. Finalization now asks what the code host can actually do: it still asks GitHub to merge when GitHub can, and where GitHub structurally cannot it waits for every check that exists to pass, brings the branch up to date, re-checks it, and merges the change itself pinned to the exact commit it verified. The report always names who merged it and how many checks passed, so a delivery resting only on the tests run on your own machine can never read as one the code host confirmed. Set `SHIPWRIGHT_ITERATE_SELF_MERGE=0` if you would rather nothing was ever merged on your behalf; a protected branch whose "Allow auto-merge" setting is merely switched off is reported rather than merged, and points you at that setting.
- **A finalization warning stopped contradicting itself.** The check that a run's hand-over note describes the run now finishing compared the two names literally while displaying them cleaned up, so any difference the cleanup removed — a stray space, an invisible character — produced a warning that named the *same* run on both sides of its own "not". It now compares exactly what it shows, through a normalizer that deliberately does not shorten long names (shortening would have turned a false warning into a false pass).
- A decision you record on a triage entry is no longer overwritten by a background check that started before you made it. The check now leaves the entry exactly as you decided it - your reason included - reports it as one it kept, and does not count it among the ones it closed (`trg-93ceb2b0`).
- `touches_build` now detects Python build inputs (`uv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `Pipfile`, `Pipfile.lock`, `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `requirements*.txt`) on BOTH surfaces — the diff-driven path patterns and the message-keyword patterns that are what actually fire at Step E. Previously the list was JS-only, so a dependency change in a Python repo raised no risk flag. Matching is whole-filename and case-sensitive on every platform (`fnmatchcase`), and git's `core.quotePath` wrapping is stripped before the basename.
- A pull request no longer stays blocked by a code review the automated reviewer has already withdrawn. When its later review passes, the reviewer now retracts its own earlier change-requests about commits that are no longer there — previously those stayed on the pull request forever, holding it back with every check green and nothing on the page saying why.
- Campaign sub-iterates now get the spec-compliance review they were promised: the autonomous loop runs the delegated cascade at step 3f-bis, after the sub-iterate is recorded and before its PR merges, so a REJECT can still stop delivery. ADR-029 named the orchestrator the delegate in May and never gave it a step.
- A campaign sub-iterate now runs the same finalization phases as a standalone iterate. The runner contract was missing the end-to-end gate (F0.5), the architecture-doc update (F2), reflection (F3a) and the test-results producer (F5) that the completeness and surface gates read — and its F4 step told every sub-iterate to append to CHANGELOG.md, which the reference forbids.
- The rule that a code review cannot be recorded as done unless the spec review passed first would have stopped firing silently once the `gates` object was no longer written. It now keys on whether the spec row is present at all, in either location.
- dev_server resolves its `shared/scripts` path once at import using `os.path` instead of lazily with `pathlib`, so a test that fakes `os.name` can no longer make the executable-resolution proxy raise `NotImplementedError`. This is the deterministic defect behind the `f0-race:shared/tests` card — it looked intermittent only because the failure needed a process where nothing had already called the proxy, which pytest-xdist decides per worker.
- The triage outbox sweep no longer deletes an append made while it commits: the GC survivor set is computed from a re-read taken after the commit window, not from the read taken before it. The window spans the drift adoption's own write, `git add`, `git diff --cached` and a `git commit` budgeted 120 s, and the sweep previously reported success while losing the line.
- Reading the triage store no longer crashes on an interrupted append: `read_text_verbatim`, `has_header`, `append_quarantine` and the reconcile read decode with `surrogateescape`, matching the reader that already documented why. `ends_without_newline` now fails closed, so an I/O error can no longer be read as "safely appendable" and concatenate two records onto one line.
- Git calls inside the triage store's locked sections are bounded and report a timeout instead of raising: new `lib.git_base.run_git_soft` returns exit 124, and each site takes its own fail-safe direction. Previously `run_git` killed the process, stranding `.git/index.lock` — in `reconcile_triage` and `sweep_drift`, in the operator's own repository — and aborted `setup_iterate_worktree` after the worktree was created.
- The F1 artifact-drift check no longer reports a git failure as a clean tree: `artifact_sync` routes through `run_git` (utf-8, bounded, kill+reap), checks the return code, and emits an explicit `error` that `finalize_bundle` maps to `failed`. A repo with too little history to compare stays a non-blocking no-op, so a greenfield first iterate can still finalize; an unknown or malformed ref stays loud.
- The triage schema-header bootstrap is durable and byte-preserving: it writes through `durable_atomic_write` instead of truncate-then-write on the git-tracked store, and prepends bytes rather than round-tripping text, so a `merge=union` artifact no longer picks up a whole-file line-ending diff. The tracked log is now written with `newline=""` like the outbox.
- `check_plugin_cache_sync.py` now resolves each plugin's cache directory from `installed_plugins.json` — the same file `update-marketplace.sh` writes into — instead of picking the highest SemVer directory present. A stale surviving version dir could otherwise make the sync report "up to date" while `--strict` reported drift with every repo file missing. Where the manifest cannot be read the highest version is still used, but the run now says so (`version_basis`) on both the ok and the drift line.
- The SessionStart plugin-cache self-heal now repairs a *partially* reaped cache. Both the shared/ tree and the 14 cross-plugin mirrors were judged from a single sentinel file each, so a reap that left that one file standing read as healthy and was never repaired — the exact failure the hook exists to catch. Each tree is now compared file-by-file against its repair source.
- A stale symlinked plugin mirror is no longer written through into an older installed version's directory (POSIX, where update-marketplace.sh links the mirror rather than copying it).
- The plugin-cache check no longer reports every plugin as out of date. It was counting a file the sync deliberately never copies, so the warning could not be cleared by re-syncing - and a genuine problem would have been lost among the false ones.
- **The plugin-cache check now verifies the shared tree it always claimed to cover, and says what a green means.** `scripts/check_plugin_cache_sync.py` compared only `plugins/shipwright-*` while its docstring promised `shared/scripts/` too, so `--strict` printed "all 14 plugin(s) in sync" without reading a byte of the cached `shared/` tree — 1005 files, including the 55 modules the F11 finalization verifier imports and every parallel iterate runs from. A *partial* reap of that verifier directory kills F11 with `ModuleNotFoundError`, is never self-healed (the SessionStart hook judges the whole tree from one sentinel file that survives it), and used to exit 0. It now exits 1. "The tree" is also no longer guessed at: the repo side is `git ls-files` — the cache is copied from a clone holding exactly the tracked files — and the cache side walks, because it is not a git tree. That closed three further holes found in review: a seven-suffix allowlist that left `shared/templates/` 3/37 verified and `shared/prompts/` 3/9 (both read from the cache at runtime, both able to vanish under a green); a bare `build` component that hid all 29 tracked files of `plugins/shipwright-build/skills/build/`, `SKILL.md` included, from both sides; and a filesystem walk that turned six gitignored generated files into permanent phantom drift on any checkout you had actually worked in. Coverage rose by 110 files with no verdict change. The verdict now carries `verified` / `ungated` / `basis`, counts unpropagated deletions and unreadable tracked files without failing on them, and never reads a zero as agreement. The cross-plugin mirror stays ungated by decision (trg-7d1d8437). (ADR-120)
- iterate campaign mode: a sub-iterate now re-checks risk from the code it actually wrote. It classified once from the spec text before any code existed, so changes to CI workflows, Claude-Code hooks, the churn/merge resolver or dependency files raised no flag at all — the integration-coverage gate reported green without ever evaluating, and a workflow-touching unit failed its own finalization on an artifact nobody told it to produce.
- The internal path check no longer flags a file as misplaced just because its folder name ends with the same word as a folder that was moved earlier — a plugin's own tests folder, for example. Genuinely misplaced paths are still caught, including ones written at the very start of a line.
- Stop GitHub CI triage from filing unactionable high-priority cards for workflows whose authoritative state is deleted, while retaining findings when state cannot be established.
- Make behavior snapshots collect pytest node IDs reliably regardless of configured verbosity while preserving marker filters.
- The refresh's own test run no longer depends on whether the machine has a logged-in `gh` CLI. Its producer subprocess inherited the working directory and was reading the real repository over the network, which made one assertion pass locally and fail in CI.
- The two traceability gates no longer report a green skip when they cannot actually run. A missing commit, an unresolvable base ref, a git fault or a failed regeneration is now a blocking error at every complexity for removal coverage, which previously documented itself as running everywhere while concluding almost nowhere. A project that is not a git repository still skips, since that is an inapplicable context rather than a failure.
- The integration-coverage gate no longer credits another run's integration test. Its fallback to the shared test-results file is now checked against the run it belongs to, matching the three sibling checks that already did so -- on a branch behind main that file is restored to the trunk's copy, so an unattributed read saw the previous run's ledger.
- Whether a directory is a git repository is now decided without relying on English error text. git ships translations, so on a localised install a genuine non-git project produced an unrecognised message and would have been blocked outright instead of skipped.
- Replaced the Windows runtime-verifier PID signal probe with a bounded Win32 process-handle wait so liveness checks cannot interrupt their own console group.
- The CI supply-chain acknowledgement gate no longer passes when git itself fails. It used to read any non-zero result from git as "this is not a git repository" and stand down quietly, so a broken git binary, a permission or ownership refusal, or a stalled repository could wave a change past the one check whose job is to make CI trust-boundary edits impossible to skip. It now stands down only when it can confirm there is genuinely no repository, and stops with an explanation otherwise.
- An iterate entry file saved in the wrong text encoding no longer kills the whole finalization report. Such a file used to crash the check run, so the operator lost the results of every check rather than just the affected one; it is now skipped and logged like any other corrupt entry while the remaining checks still report.
- Capture tracked-tree dirtiness before grade-snapshot producers write their own artifacts, preserving the pre-write state across subprocesses.
- Bind captured dirtiness to both run and canonical Git worktree so reused run IDs and subdirectory callers cannot inherit another tree's state.
- Protect grade-snapshot dirtiness from event amendments and document its optional, fail-soft consumer contract.
- External LLM review no longer reports a truncated reply as a successful review. `lib/llm_review.py` now classifies every provider reply through the same `classify_reply` its sibling `external_review.py` already used, so a cut-off or empty answer is recorded `degraded` instead of `success` — closing a silent fail-open on shipwright-adopt's Layer-3 review gate.
- External LLM reviews are no longer cut off mid-answer. Reasoning tokens bill against the same output ceiling as the visible reply, and the hard-coded 4096 left roughly 600 characters for the review itself. The budget is now 16000 with an explicit reasoning cap on the Gemini arm; measured on a 209k-character review prompt, the visible review grew from 621 to 14134 characters and the provider reports `stop` instead of `length`.
- The checks that run on your machine before a push now use the same Python version the automated checks use. Previously the two could differ, so a change could look fine locally and then fail in CI for no reason you could see.
- The finalization check that asks whether an iterate updated its specification now looks at everything the iterate changed, instead of only the last commit. When a run had to pull in newer work from the main line first, that last commit is a merge and shows almost nothing - so the check could report that no specification was updated when one had been, and block the run until it was re-run by hand.
- Align `touches_build` message matching with diff-driven whole-filename boundaries, avoiding false flags for partial basenames such as `my-package.json` and `package.json.bak`.
- Serialized the 12-plugin SessionStart cache healer fan-out and gated dependent hooks until cache repair is complete.
- Preserve complete iterate test results as immutable run-specific evidence, fail finalization on missing or mismatched snapshots, and backfill 15 source-proven historical P1 snapshots without committing the mutable root snapshot.
- Restore concurrent F0 fleet throughput with a measured weight-11 host grant, serial integration scheduling, and synchronization-based lease race tests.
- Add bounded F0 unit lifecycle output, redacted failure-tail evidence, and descendant-safe timeout and cancellation cleanup on Windows and POSIX.
- The compliance audit's suggestion for an out-of-date evidence document now names a command that can actually bring it up to date. It previously offered only a local re-render, which cannot clear a finding measured against the committed copy, and then briefly named an internal flag the skill does not accept.
- Bloat-gate hygiene: cache_tree_compare.py back under 300 lines, and the vendored plugin-cache hook's pre-existing overage formally registered as grandfathered debt across all 13 byte-identical copies
- record_event's by-commit dedup no longer skips a grade_snapshot as a duplicate merely because it shares a work_completed event's commit sha
- Build-dashboard regeneration no longer crashes on WebUI iterates whose test-status layers (unit/integration/pgtap/e2e/smoke) are a human-readable string instead of the documented {status,passed,total} mapping.
- F11 verifier gates no longer green-SKIP on a git subprocess fault (wedged index.lock, safe.directory refusal, permission failure) inside a real repository; the last callers of the binary git_available probe are migrated to the tri-state git_context classifier and the old probe is deleted
- Triage outbox GC: a buffered finding is no longer discarded just because an OLDER version of the same entry already reached the shared branch. Entries are now matched by content rather than by id, so an updated version survives until it is itself delivered (audit finding 14).
- Triage outbox GC: a decision line that was re-written in a cosmetically different form can be cleaned up again instead of sitting in the local buffer forever, which had let that buffer grow without bound or signal (audit finding 27).
- Triage compaction: a damaged status event can no longer overwrite who dismissed an entry and why. Those are the two fields the compaction reads to decide what is machine churn, so a person's decision could be reclassified and deleted (audit finding 26).
- iterate F11 checks (S9, S10, W2) no longer silently inherit a different run's category or complexity when the current run's own iterate-entry file is corrupt -- the shared tail-fallback resolver now fails closed for the requested run instead of substituting the most recent entry
- iterate_timings measurement fold produced zero data on every run because producer-owned spans (F0 queue/active, external_review, delivery) named a top-level lifecycle parent no agent mark ever recorded; a missing ancestor is now synthesized from its children's envelope instead of being orphaned
- The handover note a run writes at finalization is no longer thrown away by the same run's merge-hygiene step, so the two checks that ask "does this note describe this run" stop reporting a staleness the run caused itself (P2.15).
- Pull requests raised from a fork now actually get the required Tier-3 review instead of a permanent failed status — the resolver looked up the PR via an endpoint that is always empty for a fork commit
- Cleared a prompt-injection scanner false positive (PY_DYNAMIC_IMPORT) by normalizing two hardcoded __import__() calls in shared/tests to top-level imports.
- shipwright-security prompt-injection scanner no longer flags a suspicious Unicode codepoint quoted for exposition inside a fenced code block or inline code span in Markdown docs
- Iterate: campaign sub-iterates now persist Step 3.4's diff-driven risk re-check result, and a new F11 gate fails the run when F5c's recorded complexity is outranked by it — closing a silent recording-integrity gap that could otherwise corrupt future complexity classification
- F0's console report no longer crashes with UnicodeEncodeError when a test unit's captured output contains a character a Windows cp1252 console can't display -- it now shows a replacement character instead of losing the summary.
- Plugin-cache version-directory comparison now ranks a SemVer release (e.g. 1.0.0) above a prerelease of the same version (e.g. 1.0.0-rc1), in both the shared helper and its vendored SessionStart hook mirror
- An unreadable `shipwright_run_config.json` no longer reads as a standalone run. It used to switch off the phase gate, accept `--force` with no recorded reason, and write no `validation_overrides` entry — then overwrite the file. Anything that advances or changes a run now stops with an actionable message naming the file, the specific defect and the way forward; display-only readers still degrade quietly.
- A run config that parses but is not a JSON object (`null`, `[]`, a number) no longer demotes a run silently or crashes with a bare `AttributeError`, a file containing only `{}` is no longer mistaken for an absent one and overwritten, and `standalone` is honoured only as the literal `true` — so `"false"` no longer reads as standalone.
- A run config saved with a UTF-8 BOM (PowerShell `Out-File -Encoding utf8`, VS Code `utf8bom`) is read normally instead of reported as corrupt at "line 1 column 1".
- triage_add.py now accepts --launch-payload / --no-launch-payload, so a manually filed triage card can carry a launch payload instead of always rendering the board's "no launch payload" warning.
- A required-check finding raised from inside an iterate worktree was written to a buffer nothing drains and then deleted with the worktree. Routing is now conditional, so the finding reaches the tracked log and ships in the pull request.
- Recovered immutable test-results evidence for two runs missed by the P1.11 backfill (triage-defer-lifecycle, grade-snapshot-dedup) before their retained worktrees are removed
- External review prompt rendering substituted placeholders in several passes, so a token appearing inside the injected diff or plan was replaced again: a diff containing `{SPEC}` spliced the whole spec into the middle of it. Substitution is now a single pass over the template and injected content stays literal.
- Triage findings are no longer stranded by a single unreadable character. A finding whose text carried a byte that is not valid UTF-8 was compared two different ways depending on whether it was read from the project's history or from the local buffer, so it never matched itself: the finding stayed in the local buffer forever and, in the worse case, the whole delivery step stopped and no findings reached the pull request at all. Both sides now read such a character the same way, and the exact original bytes are preserved.
- Triage store: an unreadable span no longer reads as absence — corruption is reported on stderr (not a suppressible warning) and carried as data on `triage_cli list --json`, and a valid record behind a damaged prefix on the same physical line is now recovered instead of discarded (IT-1 audit 21/22).
- Triage store readers go through the durable reader, so an unlocked read retries past the Windows delete-pending window the sweep's atomic publish creates instead of raising; the record-separator alphabet is measured unchanged (IT-1 audit 5).
- `triage_repair.py` no longer deletes a record the reader recovers: it now uses the same record predicate, so a valid record behind a damaged prefix survives the repair rewrite instead of being quarantined away.
- SessionStart cache repair no longer runs a full plugin-cache scan once per hook process on a loaded machine. The healer election gave up waiting for its peers after a tenth of a second — less time than a peer needs to start — so up to eleven of the twelve hooks each decided they were the one that had to check the cache. Measured on a busy machine: the check now happens exactly once, in ten runs out of ten.
- The Stop-time phase-quality and compliance audits are now keyed by the iterate's own run id instead of the raw session UUID. `resolve_run_id` gained a highest-priority source — the per-session run pointer `setup_iterate_worktree.py` already writes at B1a — so findings, per-run snapshots and mirrored triage cards are attributable to the run that produced them, and the SKIP evidence emitted by the five guarded spec checks (S2, S3, W2, S9, S10) names a real run. A pointer is honoured only when its payload names the audited session and its worktree is still live; a malformed, non-object, invalid-UTF-8 or orphaned pointer falls through to the previous resolution chain rather than raising.
- Phase-quality spec checks S9 (README freshness) and S10 (CLAUDE.md sync) no longer inherit an unrelated iterate's category when the audited run_id cannot be resolved — they now SKIP, matching sibling checks S2/S3/W2.
- The phase-gate mode reader no longer crashes the gate-policy CLI on a damaged `shipwright_run_config.json`. A config whose top-level JSON is not an object, one that is not valid UTF-8, and one nested past Python's recursion limit each now read as inert (gates stay interactive) instead of ending the process with a traceback. A config carrying a UTF-8 byte-order mark is also read correctly now, so the gate mechanism and the orchestrator agree about whether a run is being driven.
- A background producer's uncommitted `.shipwright/triage.jsonl` no longer aborts an iterate's pre-merge refresh: `ensure_current` absorbs the dirty tracked log into its own path-scoped commit before every merge attempt, so the existing churn resolver reconciles a genuine divergence instead of `git merge` refusing outright.
- triage_gc --apply no longer switches off triage delivery in silence: it reports an uncommitted compaction, names the consequence (the outbox sweep refuses main_tracked_diverged on every later iterate) and prints a runnable remedy; the new opt-in --commit folds the compaction behind the same main-tree guards reconcile_main_triage uses, refusing before it rewrites anything (audit finding 16).
- reconcile_main_triage rolls its dedup rewrite back when the commit definitively fails, so a failed fold no longer strands main diverged and the outbox sweep keeps delivering — but only when HEAD has not moved and the file is still byte-for-byte what it wrote, because restoring over another writer's append would trade a delivery outage for data loss (audit finding 16).
- apply_gc writes durably: both the log and its .bak now go through durable_atomic_write (Windows sharing-violation retries + a parent-directory fsync) and derive from a single read, and the .bak is byte-identical to what it backs up — an LF log's backup previously came back CRLF, so the recovery artifact was not a copy of the thing it backed up (audit finding 9).
- The triage dedup no longer deletes a record silently: a same-id append is collapsed keep-last only when every record in the group agrees on a normalised originalTs, and anything else — disagreeing, mixed, absent or blank — keeps every line and warns that this is probably a 32-bit id collision, naming triage_repair.py. Every caller now surfaces those warnings (audit finding 25).
- The sweep's restore of main's tracked triage log no longer overwrites an append that lands in its residual window: the live file is renamed aside atomically before git checkout recreates it, and a well-formed late append is recovered into the outbox instead of destroyed. Content it cannot classify is preserved and named rather than adopted or dropped (audit finding 23).
- Triage delivery can no longer be stopped permanently by a single un-deliverable line. The outbox sweep previously had one failure mode — block everything — for three unrelated situations: a concatenated log line (which the reader recovers, so the board looked fine while nothing shipped), a dismiss whose item lives only on local main, and a status event with no usable id. Each of these stranded the entire pending backlog in a gitignored buffer. Every case now gets a proportional outcome: a line that is not deliverable *yet* is held and retried on the next sweep, one that can never be delivered is quarantined for review, and the rest of the backlog ships either way.
- Files written through the shared durable-write primitive keep their permissions on Linux and macOS. Every rewrite used to silently replace an existing file's mode with owner-only 0600, and git never showed it because only the executable bit is tracked.
- A contended or self-nested internal file lock no longer hangs indefinitely. It now waits a bounded time and then fails with a message naming the lock and how long it waited, instead of stalling in a way that looks exactly like a dead session.
- A shared library module that imports one of its siblings now loads correctly through the shadowing-proof loader. It previously failed in plugin sessions in a way that could drop triage findings without any error being shown.
- gate_policy's read of shipwright_run_config.json now retries a Windows delete-pending concurrent-rewrite the same way the orchestrator's reader does (P2.41a), instead of misreporting inert on the first transient error.
- Durable reads now retry a byte-range-locked file on Windows even when Python reports only an errno (winerror=None), instead of raising immediately.
- The run-orchestrator's phase-tasks lock now delegates to the shared bounded, reentrant file lock instead of its own unbounded, non-reentrant implementation.
- A finished iterate's run pointer is retired at PR delivery, so a retained post-merge worktree no longer misattributes later phase-quality audits to the merged run.
- The phase-quality Stop hook re-audits a provisional stale-run-id or error finding once the run's own ledger entry appears, instead of freezing it forever.
- A test unit re-run by F0 serial retry now records the retry own wall-clock and is marked as retried; previously a twice-failing unit reported only its first attempt duration and no retry marker at all
- Triage sweep: a glued drift line on main's tracked triage log now refuses with a named remedy (triage_repair.py, plus the commit step it needs) instead of an unescapable 'not a triage producer event' stall.
- A deeply-nested triage/events log line no longer crashes the canonical triage lock (RecursionError from json.loads is now caught in dedup_triage_lines and dedup_event_lines, degrading the line to unparseable/no-id instead of propagating)
- _windows_acl.py's private-directory owner check now accepts BUILTIN\Administrators/LocalSystem in addition to the current user, fixing two Windows-only F0 test failures caused by how windows-latest provisions its runneradmin profile (trg-eed74a42)
- update-marketplace.sh now verifies the marketplace clone reached origin/main's true tip before syncing files, closing a race where a 'successful' sync could leave recently-landed files missing from the plugin cache
- Events-context coverage envelope no longer conflates entries that cannot carry a selection key with ones that should but don't -- coverage.fields.<key>.unavailable is split into not_applicable / missing, and coverage.missing_work_completed surfaces the actionable subset.
- Mandated decision_log.md readers (iterate/build/plan/project first-actions) now read decision_log_index.md first, with a named grep/offset fallback, instead of promising a complete read a single 2000-line-capped Read call cannot deliver.
- New .shipwright/planning/adr/ spec-folder files are now named by run_id (collision-proof) instead of a hand-guessed number, which had collided 15 files across 6 numbers under parallel iterates; a regenerable anti-ratchet baseline guards against new collisions.
- A mandated read-completely instruction over .shipwright/planning spec.md files now runs a coverage check and declares any file it cannot fully cover, instead of silently truncating (TC3.2).
- Pin the `review` model tier to `opus` in `shipwright_model_config.json` (PR #606 had shipped `sonnet`)
- Normalized a benign literal `__import__("subprocess")` call in a test mock to a plain import, clearing a shipwright-prompt-scan PY_DYNAMIC_IMPORT false-positive (trg-133f2ca6).
- decision-drop writes now resolve against the calling iterate's own worktree instead of the main repo's disk, so a drop written mid-iterate no longer lands outside the branch that produced it

### Security

- Marked the shared-lib loader's dynamic import_module (shipwright-compliance collectors/_lib_loader.py) as a reviewed by-design site, suppressing a semgrep non-literal-import false positive (all callers pass hardcoded module names); loader behavior unchanged.
- Bumped the Lighthouse perf-runner's transitive `brace-expansion` dependency from 2.1.0 to 2.1.2, closing CVE-2026-13149 / GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp (high) — a denial of service via exponential-time expansion of consecutive non-expanding `{}` groups. Fixed at the root rather than added to the accepted-risk register.
- Closed the five open code-scanning alerts. All were false positives — every flagged dynamic import or dispatch resolves a first-party literal — but three had a root fix and got one rather than a suppression. `fr_table_reader._sibling` now enforces a closed `_ALLOWED_SIBLINGS` set, so its scanner suppression rests on a precondition instead of a comment that a future caller could silently inherit.
- PR review: a pull request can no longer forge a file-section boundary inside its own diff. The diff is fetched as bytes with no newline translation and split on LF only, so the nine characters Python treats as line breaks and git does not can no longer manufacture a `diff --git` header — which previously let a PR hide its own changed lines behind a counterfeit header pointing at a filtered path.
- PR review: the prompt template is filled in a single pass and rejects a missing placeholder, so a file legally named `{DIFF}` can no longer push the whole diff outside the block the reviewer is told to distrust. Path names rendered to the model and to the PR comment are code-spanned, length-bounded, and stripped of control, bidi and zero-width characters.
- PR review: a pull request that leaves the reviewer with no file section — an empty diff, a body with no header, or one where every section was filtered — now fails closed with a comment naming what was withheld, instead of being handed to the model as nothing and approved.
- PR review now runs on pull requests from forks. Previously the reviewer was skipped there because GitHub withholds credentials from fork runs — and a skipped job counts as a passing required check, so those changes satisfied the gate unreviewed.
- The shipped review workflow now fails closed. A change too large to review in one pass, a reviewer that crashes, and output that will not parse each leave the gate red instead of reporting a pass.
- A change that edits the checks themselves can no longer waive its own review with the skip-pr-review label.
- Single-line fields in the triage listing are now sanitized with an inline variant that also removes newlines and tabs. The shared sanitizer deliberately preserves them for multi-line launch payloads, so a stored title or reason containing a newline could previously render as an extra, forged-looking entry row.
- The CI supply-chain fingerprint binds content for non-ASCII workflow filenames. git escapes those paths octally, so both readers reported the file absent and the fingerprint stopped depending on content — an acknowledgement of a benign workflow licensed any later edit to it.
- The PR-review path sanitiser writes its character class as `\uXXXX` escapes instead of literal bidi controls, and a test pins the matched alphabet by enumeration. Behaviour is unchanged — verified against the previous revision — but the file no longer renders its own source right-to-left, nor reports a line count Python and git disagree on.
- Bumped pyasn1 to 0.6.4 in the shipwright-plan lockfile, remediating CVE-2026-59885 and CVE-2026-59886 (denial of service when decoding crafted ASN.1 OBJECT IDENTIFIER and REAL values).
- Recorded a scoped, expiring acceptance for CVE-2026-14257 (brace-expansion) in the Trivy ignore file and the governance register: it is reachable only through the dev-only Lighthouse perf-runner, and no fixed version exists for the pinned minimatch tree.
- The PR-review path filter's reliance on git quoting non-ASCII paths is now pinned by test rather than assumed: one test records that a quoted path reaches the model carrying nothing invisible, and one fails if the diff parser ever decodes the escapes back into characters.
- iterate campaign mode: a sub-iterate that touches the CI trust boundary now stops and hands back to an operator instead of proceeding. Renaming a workflow out of the boundary is caught too — git reports only the new path by default, so disabling a security workflow by renaming it previously raised nothing.
- Route every DeepSeek OpenRouter request only through the verified novita/together US zero-retention allowlist with fallbacks and data collection disabled.
- Decided the long-open question of whether the accepted-risk register should cover inline suppressions: it should not, and the position is now enforced by a ratchet rather than only asserted in prose. `docs/security-ci-setup.md` carries the reasoning and the three disclosed limits.
- Fork-raised pull requests are reliably reviewed again: the PR-review gate's Stage 2 resolver silently failed closed on every fork PR since the two-stage split shipped

## [0.31.0] - 2026-07-12

### Added

- Single-session pipelines (`/shipwright-run` `mode: single_session`) are now resumable: if the orchestrator conversation dies mid-run, re-invoking `/shipwright-run` detects the in-flight run and offers to resume it, re-running any mid-flight phase idempotently. Recovery is available via `single-session-resume` / `-recover`.
- Structured observability for the single-session loop, appended to `.shipwright/run_loop_events.jsonl` (phase dispatch, result, strict-stop, human-gate pause/resume, resume, recovery). Multi-session runs are unaffected — no telemetry or loop-state files are created for them.
- Single-session pipeline: end-to-end integration + cross-surface regression capstone — a full pipeline walked through a human-gate pause/resume with strict-stop, cross-surface parity (CLI/WebUI/chat), in-flight multi-session resumability, and a known-bug regression roster.
- Constitution + generated-project CLAUDE.md now instruct agents to phrase questions to the user in plain, functional language a non-senior developer can understand (no unexplained jargon).
- /shipwright-adopt accepts a pre-delivered brief (`--brief`) and skips the Step-C product-description question the brief answers, mirroring /shipwright-run's brief-intake; `brief_intake.py` is now shared by both plugins.
- The /shipwright-run and /shipwright-iterate intro banners now surface plain-language explanations of jargon (IREB-Spec, ADR, Conventional Commits) drawn verbatim from the shared Plain-Language Index in docs/guide.md, with a copy-parity test guarding against drift.
- CLAUDE.md net-growth gate: the F11 verifier check_agent_doc_budget (+ standalone CLI) now blocks an iterate that grows CLAUDE.md by more than 30 lines vs the git base — forward-only, creation/deletion exempt; deliberate escape hatch SHIPWRIGHT_CLAUDE_MD_GROWTH_OK=1 is surfaced visibly, never silently
- Control Grade history: each compliance dashboard regen now appends one durable `grade_snapshot{grade, score, ts}` event to the tracked `shipwright_events.jsonl` (new `record_event` `--type grade_snapshot`), so the WebUI Ship's-Log can project a grade TREND + per-run delta instead of only the latest overwritten aggregate — additive (existing events and dashboard output unchanged; unknown-type consumers ignore it), one snapshot per regen with WebUI-side dedup
- Iterate session-plan persistence: `classify_complexity.py --run-id` now additively writes a `<run_id>.plan.json` (complexity, planned phases, skipped phases + why, risk flags) next to the finalized iterate record so the WebUI scoped Plan-Card has a data source — stdout stays byte-identical, the persist is fail-soft and the file is gitignored transient run-scoped state
- Brief-intake for `/shipwright-run`: the pipeline entry point now accepts a pre-delivered brief (a file path or an inline payload) carrying the WebUI Intent-Wizard's four plain-language answers (description, users, persistence, run-location), maps them to the stack profile and deploy/env choices (`persistence=yes` → `supabase-nextjs`; otherwise the zero-signup `vite-hono` default; Supabase env vars asked only for web + persistence), and asks ONLY the still-missing questions — so the terminal interview never re-asks what the wizard already answered — while a run WITHOUT a brief behaves exactly as before (legacy interview unchanged); a malformed, unreadable or wrong-shape brief degrades to the legacy interview rather than failing the run (K2c)
- Iterate-Rail per-phase durations: the iterate finalize step now folds real per-group wall-clock times (scope/build/review/test/finalize) into its `work_completed` event as `phase_timings`, so the Command Center's iterate progress bar can show time-per-step (the iterate counterpart of the pipeline PhaseRail; additive, older runs are unaffected).
- Pipeline event log now records a `phase_started` at each **pipeline phase's** entry and a paired `phase_completed`/`phase_failed` at its exit, in both run modes — multi-session via the SessionStart/Stop hooks, single-session (the default) via the `single-session-next`/`single-session-apply` boundaries — so per-phase durations are computable from the tracked `shipwright_events.jsonl` alone (WebUI PhaseRail, M-Pre-1). Additive and best-effort. Per-phase timing for the iterate flow (F0–F12) is tracked separately as a follow-up.

### Changed

- /shipwright-run: single_session is now the default and sole supported pipeline mode (runs on every surface); multi_session is deprecated (retained for back-compat, removal deferred). Existing runs migrate explicitly — see docs/migrations/multi-session-to-single-session.md.
- Both CLAUDE.md producers (greenfield template and /shipwright-adopt renderer) now seed an 'Editing this file (keep it lean)' rule — one line + ADR pointer per invariant, rationale in the ADR — so generated projects stop accreting inline-rationale DO-NOT blocks
- Design feedback rounds (design-feedback-round*.md) are now gitignored transient scratch; the design review-loop documents how the single-session WebUI hosts the emitted review viewer (isolated, not the Smart Viewer) and writes feedback straight into the worktree
- Phase events in shipwright_events.jsonl now carry a top-level splitId and phase_completed is deduplicated on (phase, splitId), enabling per-split duration bars in the WebUI PhaseRail. Single-split phases are unchanged.

### Fixed

- shipwright-iterate intro banner no longer prints a hardcoded, stale version string (was `v0.3.0`); the banner now carries no version, matching every other plugin, with a drift-guard test preventing regression.
- External LLM review no longer errors on the direct OpenAI call under gpt-5.x models (max_tokens replaced by max_completion_tokens in external_review.py and llm_review.py).
- The external review gate no longer silently falls back to self-review when API keys are present but every review fails: it now returns a non-zero exit with a recorded degraded reason instead of a success-looking result.
- Multi-split pipeline phases (build/plan) no longer undercount their duration in the tracked event log: every split's completion is recorded, so the per-phase span spans the whole phase instead of only the first split.
- Iterate finalization no longer loses its history entry when a gitignored WebUI session-plan card (<run_id>.plan.json) is present: the iterate-entry reader now excludes secondary-extension sidecars, so retention can't mistake the date-less plan file for the oldest entry and delete the real one (and find_entry_by_run_id resolves the real entry).

## [0.30.0] - 2026-07-08

**Highlights** — the largest release since v0.29: a month of parallel campaigns, now consolidated.

- **New `shipwright-grade` plugin** — a read-only, deterministic Control-Grade (A–F) grader for any git repository (local path or clone-from-URL) that reuses the compliance scoring engine; underivable dimensions render an honest `n/a`.
- **Diff-coverage CI gate is now hard-blocking** — changed-line coverage below 80% blocks merge, routed through one shared composite action; adopted repos get a warn-only starter gate.
- **Single-session pipeline mode** — `/shipwright-run` can drive the whole pipeline in one session via a phase-runner subagent, non-interactive phase gates, and guaranteed artifact persistence.
- **Security & Control-Grade hardening** — CodeQL/Semgrep/Trivy tailored with scoped, time-bounded accepted-risk registers, least-privilege workflow tokens, and a plain-language, open-standard-anchored compliance dashboard (BP-1/BP-2 FR-mapping, AR-05 RTM, AR-10 SARIF ingestion, SBOM honesty).

### Added

- shipwright-security: Trivy SCA findings can now be accepted via a repo-root `.trivyignore.yaml` (scoped by path, time-bounded via `expired_at`), wired through `--ignorefile` keyed to the scanned target so it works in CI, locally, and adopted repos. First use: the non-reachable OTel medium (CVE-2026-54285) in the Lighthouse perf-runner is accepted with a 2026-12-22 re-review.
- shipwright-security self-scan: a CI guardrail now enforces that every `.trivyignore.yaml` accepted-risk entry is scoped (paths/purls), time-bounded (`expired_at`), and justified (`statement`) — a sloppy or blanket suppression fails CI. Makes the accept-discipline mechanical, not just convention.
- compliance: Control Verdict + Control Grade (A–F) block atop the dashboard — a deterministic, importance-weighted control-posture grade (lib/control_grade.py, methodology in Anlehnung an OpenSSF Scorecard; dimensions anchored to SLSA, NIST SSDF, DO-178C/RTM, OWASP, ISO 25010), reusable by the future repo-grader and certification gate
